\displaystyle\nabla^{2}(f\circ\varphi)=(\nabla\varphi)^{t}\cdot\left((\nabla^{2}f)\circ\varphi\right)\cdot(\nabla\varphi)+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left((\partial_{x_{k}}f)\circ\varphi\right)\,\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}.

(9.24)

Let
\iota:\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
be the inversion, given for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
by

\displaystyle\iota(x)=\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}=\left(\frac{x_{1}}{|x|^{2}},\frac{x_{2}}{|x|^{2}},\frac{x_{3}}{|x|^{2}},\frac{x_{4}}{|x|^{2}}\right)

We have

\displaystyle\nabla\iota

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\vskip 5.0pt\dfrac{1}{|x|^{2}}-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}x_{2}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}\\
\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}\,x_{2}}{|x|^{4}}&\dfrac{1}{|x|^{2}}-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}\\
\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{4}}&\dfrac{1}{|x|^{2}}-\dfrac{2\,x_{3}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{3}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}\\
\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{3}x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}&\dfrac{1}{|x|^{4}}-\dfrac{2\,x_{4}^{2}}{|x|^{6}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|x|^{4}}\begin{pmatrix}-x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}&-2\,x_{1}\,x_{2}&-2\,x_{1}\,x_{3}&-2\,x_{1}\,x_{4}\\
-2\,x_{1}\,x_{2}&x_{1}^{2}-x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}&-2\,x_{2}\,x_{3}&-2\,x_{2}\,x_{4}\\
-2\,x_{1}\,x_{3}&-2\,x_{2}\,x_{3}&x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}&-2\,x_{3}\,x_{4}\\
-2\,x_{1}\,x_{4}&-2\,x_{2}\,x_{4}&-2\,x_{3}\,x_{4}&x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}\end{pmatrix}

Therefore, if
v=u\circ\iota
we get (writing by abuse of notation
\partial_{x_{i}}u
for
\left(\partial_{x_{i}}u\right)\circ\iota
)

\displaystyle|\nabla v|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|x|^{8}}\bigg(\left(-x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)^{2}(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{2}^{2}(\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{3}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{3}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{4}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{4}}u)^{2}

\displaystyle+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{2}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}-x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)^{2}(\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{2}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{3}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{2}^{2}\,x_{4}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{4}}u)^{2}

\displaystyle+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{3}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{2}^{2}\,x_{3}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)(\partial_{x_{3}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{3}^{2}\,x_{4}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{4}}u)^{2}

\displaystyle+4\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{4}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{1}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{2}^{2}\,x_{4}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{2}}u)^{2}+4\,x_{3}^{2}\,x_{4}^{2}\,(\partial_{x_{3}}u)^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}\right)^{2}(\partial_{x_{4}}u)^{2}

\displaystyle-4\,x_{1}\,x_{2}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u\,\partial_{x_{2}}u\big(\left(-x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)+\left(x_{1}^{2}-x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)-2\,x_{3}^{2}-2\,x_{4}^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-4\,x_{1}\,x_{3}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u\,\partial_{x_{3}}u\big(\left(-x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)-2\,x_{2}^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)-2\,x_{4}^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-4\,x_{1}\,x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\big(\left(-x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)-2\,x_{2}^{2}-2\,x_{3}^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}\right)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-4\,x_{2}\,x_{3}\,\partial_{x_{2}}u\,\partial_{x_{3}}u\big(-2\,x_{1}^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}-x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)-2\,\,x_{4}^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-4\,x_{2}\,x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{3}}u\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\big(-2\,x_{1}^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}-x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)-2\,x_{3}^{2}+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}\right)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle-4\,x_{3}\,x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{3}}u\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\big(-2\,x_{1}^{2}-2\,x_{2}^{2}+\left(+x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}-x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}\right)+\left(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}-x_{4}^{2}\right)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{4}}.

In particular, for all
u\in W^{1,4}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}|\nabla v|^{4}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}|\nabla u|^{4}dx

(9.25)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{|\nabla v|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

Now, we compute

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\iota_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{6\,x_{1}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{3}}{|x|^{6}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{2}}{|x|^{6}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{3}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{6}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{6}}\\
\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{2\,x_{2}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{2}}{|x|^{6}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{2}^{2}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{6}}\\
\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{2\,x_{3}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{6}}&-\dfrac{2\,x_{1}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{3}^{2}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{3}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{6}}\\
\vskip 5.0pt-\dfrac{2\,x_{4}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}^{2}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{8\,x_{1}x_{2}\,x_{3}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{3}\,x_{4}}{|x|^{6}}&\dfrac{-2\,x_{1}}{|x|^{4}}+\dfrac{8\,x_{1}\,x_{4}^{2}}{|x|^{6}}\end{pmatrix},

with circular formulae for
\nabla^{2}\iota_{i}
for
i=2,3,4
. In particular, we find that

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}\iota_{k}(x)|\leq\frac{8}{|x|^{3}}\qquad\text{for all}\;\,1\leq i,j,k\leq 4.

Therefore, (
9.24
) implies that

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}v|\leq\frac{4}{|x|^{4}}\left(|\nabla^{2}u|+2|x||\nabla u|^{2}\right).

Otherwise, one can prove directly that

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}=\frac{1}{|x|^{8}}\Big(|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+8\left(x_{1}^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)|\partial_{x_{1}}u|^{2}+8\left(x_{2}^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)|\partial_{x_{2}}u|^{2}

\displaystyle+8\left(x_{3}^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)|\partial_{x_{3}}u|^{2}+8\left(x_{4}^{2}+|x|^{2}\right)|\partial_{x_{4}}u|^{2}+16\sum_{i,j=1}^{4}x_{i}\,x_{j}\,\partial_{x_{i}}u\,\partial_{x_{j}}u+8\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{4}\partial_{x_{i},x_{j}}^{2}u\,x_{i}\,\partial_{x_{j}}u

\displaystyle+4\,\partial_{x_{1}}^{2}u\left(x_{1}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-x_{2}\,\partial_{x_{2}}u-x_{3}\,\partial_{x_{3}}u-x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\right)+4\,\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}u\left(-x_{1}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u+x_{2}\,\partial_{x_{2}}u-x_{3}\,\partial_{x_{3}}u-x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\right)

\displaystyle+4\,\partial_{x_{3}}^{2}u\left(-x_{1}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-x_{2}\,\partial_{x_{2}}u+x_{3}\,\partial_{x_{3}}u-x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\right)+4\,\partial_{x_{4}}^{2}u\left(-x_{1}\,\partial_{x_{1}}u-x_{2}\,\partial_{x_{2}}u-x_{3}\,\partial_{x_{3}}u+x_{4}\,\partial_{x_{4}}u\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|x|^{8}}\Big(|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+8|x|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+8|x\cdot\nabla u|^{2}+8\,x^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot(\nabla u)-4(x\cdot\nabla u)\Delta u\Big).

In particular, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}v|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{|x|^{8}}\left(5|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+12|x|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}+12|x\cdot\nabla u|^{2}+|\Delta u|^{2}\right)\leq\frac{3}{|x|^{8}}\left(3|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}+8|x|^{2}|\nabla u|^{2}\right).

Before stating the extension lemma, we need an elementary estimate of the Poincaré-Wirtinger constant.

Theorem 9.12
.

Let
d\geq 3
,
0<r<\infty
and
\Omega_{r}=B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)
. Then, for all
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{r})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{r}}\frac{\left|u-\overline{u}_{\Omega_{r}}\right|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq\frac{4}{(d-2)^{2}}\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{r}}\left|u-\overline{u}_{\Omega_{r}}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{16\,r^{2}}{(d-2)^{2}}\int_{\Omega_{r}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

Proof.

By an immediate scaling argument, it suffices to check the case
r=1
. Consider the following minimisation problem

\displaystyle\mu_{d}=\inf\left\{\int_{\Omega_{1}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx:\int_{\Omega_{1}}u\,dx=0,\;\,\int_{\Omega_{1}}|u|^{2}dx=1\right\}.

It yields the optimal constant in the Poincaré-Wirtinger inequality, but we will consider another simpler problem that gives a near-optimal constant

\displaystyle\mu_{d}^{\ast}=\inf\left\{\int_{\Omega_{1}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx:\int_{\Omega_{1}}u\,dx=0,\;\,\int_{\Omega_{1}}\frac{|u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=1\right\}.

By standard methods of the calculus of variations, there exists a minimiser
u
that satisfies the following system of equations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} -\Delta u&=\mu_{d}^{\ast}\frac{u}{|x|^{2}}\qquad&&\text{in}\;\,\Omega_{1}\\
\partial_{\nu}u&=0\qquad&&\text{on}\;\,\partial\Omega_{1}\\
\int_{\Omega_{1}}u\,dx&=0\\
\int_{\Omega_{1}}\frac{u^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx&=1.\end{aligned}\right.

Expand
u
in spherical harmonics

\displaystyle u(r,\omega)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{d}(n)}u_{n,k}(r)Y_{n}^{k}(\omega).

The Euler-Lagrange equation implies that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq k\leq N_{d}(n)
, we have

\displaystyle u_{n,k}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{d-1}{r}u_{n,k}^{\prime}(r)-\frac{n(n+d-2)}{r^{2}}u_{n,k}(r)=-\mu_{d}^{\ast}u_{n,k}.

Making the change of variable
u_{n,k}(r)=Y_{n,k}(\log(r))
and removing the indices for simplicity, we deduce that

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime}+(d-2)Y^{\prime}-n(n+d-2)Y=-\mu_{d}^{\ast}Y.

The discriminant of the characteristic polynomial
P(X)=X^{2}+(d-2)X-n(n+d-2)+\mu_{d}^{\ast}
is given by

\displaystyle D=(d-2)^{2}+4n(n+d-2)-4\mu_{d}^{\ast}.

If
D\geq 0
, we easily see that the boundary condition
Y^{\prime}=0
on
\partial[0,\log(2)]
cannot be satisfied, which implies that
D<0
or

\displaystyle\mu_{d}^{\ast}>n(n+d-2)+\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}.

Therefore, we obtain the elementary estimate
\mu_{d}^{\ast}>\dfrac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}
, which implies that for all
u\in W^{1,2}(\Omega_{1})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1}}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\geq\frac{(d-2)^{2}}{4}\int_{\Omega_{1}}\frac{|u-\overline{u}_{\Omega_{1}}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx,

and finally, the second inequality follows from the elementary estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1}}\frac{|u-\overline{u}_{\Omega_{1}}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\geq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Omega_{1}}\left|u-\overline{u}_{\Omega_{1}}\right|^{2}dx,

which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Now, let us state the first extension theorem, inspired from
[
25
, Lemma C.1]
.

Theorem 9.13
.

There exists a universal constant
0<\Gamma_{\mathrm{W}}<\infty
with the following property.
Let
0<2\,a<b<\infty
and let
\Omega=B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
. Let
u\in W^{2,2}(\Omega)
. Then, there exists an extension
\widetilde{u}\in W^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 19\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+289\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 7\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq\sqrt{51}\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 196\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+28\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 261\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+4046\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 98\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+14\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 261\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+4046\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 98\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+14\sqrt[4]{2}\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}.\end{aligned}\right.

(9.26)

In particular, as
\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(U)}\leq\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(U)}
and
\left\|\dfrac{\,\cdot\,}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(U)}\leq 2\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(U)}
for all open subset
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, we deduce that the three norms

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} N_{2,4}&=\left\|\nabla^{2}(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\\
N_{2,2}&=\left\|\nabla^{2}(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\dfrac{\nabla(\,\cdot\,)}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\\
N_{2,(4,2)}&=\left\|\nabla^{2}(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega)}\end{aligned}\right.

(9.27)

are mutually equivalent on
W^{2,2}(\Omega)/\mathbb{R}
and that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\max\left\{N_{2,2}(u),N_{2,(4,2)}(u)\right\}\leq\Gamma_{\mathrm{W}}\,N_{2,4}(u)\\
&\max\left\{N_{2,4}(u),N_{2,(4,2)}(u)\right\}\leq\Gamma_{\mathrm{W}}\,N_{2,2}(u).\end{aligned}\right.

(9.28)

Remark 9.14
.

Explicitly, we can take

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{W}}=28\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)=98705.182\cdots,

Proof.

Step 1: Weighted Gradient Estimate.

Let
\chi\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R})
such that
0\leq\chi\leq 1
,
\chi=1
on
[0,1]
and
\mathrm{supp}(\chi)\subset[0,2]
. By smoothing the function

\displaystyle\eta(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &0\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,t\leq-2\\
&2+t\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,-2\leq t\leq-1\\
&1\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,-1\leq t\leq 1\\
&2-t\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,1\leq t\leq 2\\
&0\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,t\geq 2\end{aligned}\right.

that satisfies
|\eta^{\prime}|\leq 1
on
\mathbb{R}\setminus\left\{-2,-1,1,2\right\}
, we can assume that

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|\chi^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})},\left\|\chi^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\right\}\leq 2.

(9.29)

Then, for all
r>0
let
\chi_{r}\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
be such that
\chi_{r}(x)=\eta\left(\dfrac{|x|}{r}\right)
. We have

\displaystyle\nabla\chi_{r}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r}\frac{x}{|x|}\eta^{\prime}\left(\frac{|x|}{r}\right)

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\chi_{r}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r|x|^{3}}\begin{pmatrix}x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}&-x_{1}\,x_{2}&-x_{1}\,x_{3}&-x_{1}\,x_{4}\\
-x_{1}\,x_{2}&x_{1}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}&-x_{2}\,x_{3}&-x_{2}\,x_{4}\\
-x_{1}\,x_{3}&-x_{2}\,x_{3}&x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{4}^{2}&-x_{3}\,x_{4}\\
-x_{1}\,x_{4}&-x_{2}\,x_{4}&-x_{3}\,x_{4}&x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\eta^{\prime}\left(\frac{|x|}{r}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r|x|^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}^{2}&x_{1}\,x_{2}&x_{1}\,x_{3}&x_{1}\,x_{4}\\
x_{1}\,x_{2}&x_{2}^{2}&x_{2}\,x_{3}&x_{2}\,x_{4}\\
x_{1}\,x_{3}&x_{2}\,x_{3}&x_{3}^{2}&x_{3}\,x_{4}\\
x_{1}\,x_{4}&x_{2}\,x_{4}&x_{3}\,x_{4}&x_{4}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\eta^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{|x|}{r}\right).

Therefore, we get by (
9.29
)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &|\nabla\chi_{r}|\leq\frac{2}{r}\mathbf{1}_{B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}\\
&|\nabla^{2}\chi_{r}|\leq\frac{4}{r|x|}\mathbf{1}_{B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}.\end{aligned}\right.

(9.30)

Define

\displaystyle\overline{u}(x)=\chi_{a}(x)u(x)+\left(1-\chi_{r}(x)\right){\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.57222pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.91281pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.30664pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.13547pt}}\!\int}_{\mkern-10.0muB_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}u\,d\mathscr{L}^{4}=\chi_{a}(x)u(x)+\left(1-\chi_{r}(x)\right)\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}.

We have

\displaystyle\nabla\overline{u}=\chi_{a}\nabla u+\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla\chi_{a}.

(9.31)

Thanks to Theorem
9.12
and (
9.30
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{\left|\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla\chi_{a}\right|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{\left|u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq\frac{16}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq 16\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nabla\overline{u}}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\chi_{a}\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+\left\|\frac{\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)}{|x|}\nabla\chi_{a}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq 5\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))},

or

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq 25\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

(9.32)

Now, consider the function

\displaystyle\widehat{u}(x)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{aligned} &u(x)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)\\
&\overline{u}\left(a^{2}\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)\qquad&&\text{for all}\;\,x\in B_{a}(0)\end{aligned}\right.

By conformal invariance (
9.25
), we deduce by (
9.32
) that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla\widehat{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx=\int_{\mathbb{C}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\leq 25\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

(9.33)

Likewise, using the same construction on
B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0)
yields a function
\widetilde{u}\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
such that
\mathrm{supp}(\widetilde{u})\subset B_{2b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{a}{2}}(0)
, and using (
9.33
)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq 51\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\\
&\int_{B_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq 25\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\\
&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{b}(0)}\frac{|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq 25\int_{B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.\end{aligned}\right.

(9.34)

Step 2: Hessian Estimate.

Now, let us estimate the
L^{2}
norm of
\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{a}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\widehat{u}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\left(|\nabla^{2}\overline{u}|^{2}+8\frac{|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}+8\left|\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla\overline{u}\right|^{2}+8\,\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\right)^{t}\cdot\nabla^{2}u\cdot\nabla u-4\left(\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\cdot\nabla u\right)\Delta u\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq 9\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla^{2}\overline{u}|^{2}dx+24\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla\overline{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

(9.35)

We have

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\overline{u}=\chi_{a}\nabla^{2}u+2\nabla u\cdot\nabla\chi_{a}+\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla^{2}\chi_{a}.

(9.36)

Using (
9.30
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla u\cdot\nabla\chi_{a}|^{2}dx\leq 4\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

(9.37)

and Theorem
9.12
shows that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\left|\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla^{2}\chi_{a}\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{\left|u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{64}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}dx\leq 64\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx.

(9.38)

Therefore, we have by (
9.36
), (
9.37
), and (
9.5
)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\overline{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+8\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+8\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+16\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

(9.39)

and by (
9.5
) and (
9.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\widehat{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{a}(0))}\leq 3\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+80\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.40)

Likewise, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{b}(0))}\leq 3\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0))}+80\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0))}.

(9.41)

Therefore, we finally deduce by the inversion estimate (
9.5
), and the estimates (
9.40
) and (
9.41
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 7\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+161\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(9.42)

Step 3: Gradient Lorentz-Sobolev Estimate.

Now, we have by (
9.42
) and the improved Sobolev inequality from Theorem
9.11

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 14\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 98\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+2254\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(9.43)

which shows by the extension property of
\widetilde{u}
that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega)}\leq 261\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+4046\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(9.44)

Notice also that thanks to Proposition
9.1

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}\leq\left(\frac{4}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega)}\leq 261\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+4046\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

On the other hand, the
L^{2,1}/L^{2,\infty}
duality, the doubling estimate of Lemma
9.6
, and (
9.21
) show that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq\left\||\nabla u|^{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,1}(\Omega)}\left\|\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2,\infty}(\Omega)}\leq 4\pi\sqrt{2}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

(9.45)

Step 4: Biquadratic Estimate for the Gradient.

Now, let us estimate the
L^{4}
norm of the extension. We first need an elementary variant of the Poincaré-Sobolev inequality.

Theorem 9.15
.

There exists a universal constant
\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}<\infty
such that for all
r>0
and
u\in W^{2,2}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\left\|u-\overline{u}_{B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}\leq\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\left(\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}+r\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2r}\setminus\overline{B}_{r}(0))}\right)

(9.46)

Proof.

An immediate scaling argument shows that we need only establish the inequality for
r=1
. Write for simplicity
A_{1}=B_{2}\setminus\overline{B}_{1}(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
. We argue by contradiction, and let
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset W^{2,2}(A)
such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \int_{A_{1}}|u_{k}|^{4}dx&=1\\
\int_{A_{1}}u_{k}\,dx&=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(9.47)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\left\|\nabla^{2}u_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{1})}+\left\|\nabla u_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{1})}\right)=0.

Thanks to the Sobolev embedding
\displaystyle W^{2,2}(A_{1})\lhook\joinrel\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow W^{1,4}(A_{1})\lhook\joinrel\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\bigcap_{q<\infty}L^{q}(A_{1})
, the sequence
\left\{u_{k}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is precompact in
L^{4}(A_{1})
and
L^{1}(A_{1})
, and up to a subsequence, we deduce that
u_{k}\hookrightarrow u_{\infty}\in W^{2,2}(A_{1})
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}u_{\infty}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{1})}+\left\|\nabla u_{\infty}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{1})}\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left(\left\|\nabla^{2}u_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{1})}+\left\|\nabla u_{k}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(A_{1})}\right)=0.

while (by compactness)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \int_{A_{1}}u_{\infty}\,dx&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

(9.48)

Therefore, we deduce that
u_{\infty}
is constant, and since
u_{\infty}
has vanishing mean, this implies that
u_{\infty}
, which contradicts the first equation in (
9.48
).
∎

Using Theorem (
9.15
) and the pointwise estimate (
9.30
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla\chi_{a}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{a}\left\|u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\left(\frac{1}{a}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\right).

(9.49)

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{a^{2}}\sqrt{2\pi^{2}\left((2a)^{4}-a^{4}\right)}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}=\pi\sqrt{30}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.50)

By (
9.5
) and (
9.50
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla\chi_{a}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\leq 2\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\left(\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+\sqrt[4]{30}\sqrt{\pi}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\right).

(9.51)

Then, we trivially have

\displaystyle\left\|\chi_{a}\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\leq\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.52)

Combining (
9.31
), (
9.51
), and (
9.52
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\overline{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\leq 2\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+\left(1+2\,\sqrt[4]{30}\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.53)

Therefore, (
9.25
) and (
9.53
) show that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widehat{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{a}(0))}=\left\|\nabla\overline{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\leq 2\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+\left(1+2\,\sqrt[4]{30}\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.54)

Therefore, we finally get an extension
\widetilde{u}\in W^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq\left(1+4\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\right)\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left(3+4\sqrt[4]{30}\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma_{\mathrm{PS}}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}.

(9.55)

Now, let us get a new bound for the Hessian (involving the
L^{4}
norm). We have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\chi_{a}\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\leq\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\left\|\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(2\pi^{2}\log(2)\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))},

and likewise

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{\left|\left(u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right)\nabla\chi_{a}\right|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\frac{\left|u-\overline{u}_{B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0)}\right|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\leq\frac{16}{a^{2}}\int_{B_{2a}\setminus B_{a}(0)}|\nabla u|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16}{a^{2}}\sqrt{2\pi^{2}\left((2a)^{4}-a^{4}\right)}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq 16\pi\sqrt{30}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}\leq\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\sqrt{\pi}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.56)

Therefore, (
9.5
) and (
9.56
) show that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+80\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}

\displaystyle\leq 3\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}+80\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{2a}\setminus\overline{B}_{a}(0))}.

(9.57)

Likewise, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\setminus\overline{B}_{b}(0))}\leq 3\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0))}+80\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(B_{b}\setminus\overline{B}_{\frac{b}{2}}(0))},

and finally, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 7\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}.

(9.58)

Therefore, reusing the Sobolev inequality from Theorem
9.23
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 14\left\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 98\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+14\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega)}\leq 98\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+14\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)},

and by Proposition
9.1
, we also have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq 98\sqrt[4]{2}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+14\sqrt[4]{2}\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)},

(9.59)

while (
9.45
) shows that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\frac{|\nabla\widetilde{u}|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left\|\nabla\widetilde{u}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}

\displaystyle\leq 196\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+28\sqrt[4]{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left(1+160\left(\sqrt[4]{2\log(2)}+4\sqrt[4]{30}\right)\right)\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)},

(9.60)

which concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

We see that on a neck region, there are three equivalent norms on
W^{2,2}(\Omega)/\mathbb{R}
(and that the stronger norm involving
L^{4,2}
always controls the other two for any domain), but for technical reasons, it will be more convenient to choose an
L^{2}
norm, that is

\displaystyle N_{2,2}(u)=\left\|\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\frac{\nabla u}{|x|}\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}=\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\int_{\Omega}\frac{|\nabla u|^{2}}{|x|^{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which corresponds to an
“intermediate”
norm between
N_{2,4}=\left\|\nabla^{2}(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4}(\Omega)}
and
N_{2,(4,2)}=\left\|\nabla^{2}(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla(\,\cdot\,)\right\|_{\mathrm{L}^{4,2}(\Omega)}
.
